ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u12	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position towards the Government's draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act, the Tax Ordination Act and some other laws, prints No. 2860 and 2918. I must point out that the bill is a very large set of rules, containing more than 140 articles and covering more than 400 pages. To a large extent, this proposal introduces tax regulations in connection with the guidelines of the Council of the European Union Directive 2016/1164 of 12 July 2016 laying down provisions to combat tax avoidance practices which have a direct impact on the functioning of the internal market of a given country, so-called. ATAD Directive. This project is part of a package of several already introduced laws by the Law and Justice Government aimed primarily at sealing the tax system, in particular as regards income taxes, both PIT and CIT, and increasing the revenue of the state budget. It should be noted that the project introduces inter alia. the long-awaited exit tax, i.e. the unrealised capital gains tax. It aims to counter tax avoidance by transferring assets, tax residences or the entire permanent establishment to another country. This tax will cover both companies and individuals. Such a tax or tax already exists in several European Union countries, so that it is not new that such taxes are being applied. The project also introduces a legitimate obligation to report tax schemes. The point is to avoid aggressive tax optimizations. In particular, tax advisors, legal advisers, bank workers and experts will be included in this reporting requirement. This information, these reports will be submitted to the head of the National Tax Administration. What is important and worth noting, in this project, the government proposes a very low tax, a 5% CIT rate for eligible IPR income. If an entrepreneur introduces such solutions, in research and development, in manufacturing, developing, improving his production, he will pay only a 5% tax. The proposal also introduces new rules on taxation of crypto-February trading, so taxation is expected to operate from 2019. It also clarifies and simplifies transfer pricing rules. During the discussion in the committee there were many doubts and comments on the need for clarification, clarification of many provisions, and, as I said, there are more than 140 of these articles. The Law and Justice Club therefore proposes 14 amendments to clarify, clarify the provisions of the Act. As regards the remaining issues, the Law and Justice Club fully supports the draft Print Act No 2918. I'm handing over amendments on behalf of the club. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u13	Thank you, sir. And I invite Mr Włodzimierz Nykiel to present a report to the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! For the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] I have the honour to present an opinion on the draft law on the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act, the Tax Law and some other laws, print no. 2918, previously - no. 2860. The most important regulations proposed in the project concern income taxes, i.e. one group, and, the second group, issues subject to the Tax Order, i.e. general tax law. There are other changes, but these certainly belong to the most important ones. The draft provides for the introduction of rules on income tax on exit tax in the income tax laws. This tax is to be imposed on taxable persons in the event of a transfer of assets to another country or a change in the tax residence. It will be subject to both legal persons and natural persons. These changes are, to some extent, an implementation of the so-called. The Anti-Tax Avoidance Directive counteracting tax avoidance, however, the projector decided to tighten the tax conditions under the ATAD Directive. First of all, natural persons have been taxed, whereas this directive does not provide for such taxation. In addition, the proposal provides for taxation regardless of whether emigration is intended to abuse tax law or is dictated by other considerations. The project's distribution into instalments may be considered incompatible with EU law. A better solution would be to defer the direct tax, which would consist in the obligation to pay it only at the time of the actual disposal of the assets. Such a solution exists, among others. in France, Spain, the Netherlands, Germany and Norway, it should be followed. I will put forward an amendment in this regard. A number of rules on taxation of income from virtual currencies, preferential rules on taxation of income related to intellectual property rights or changes in the transfer pricing sphere deserve a positive assessment. A large group of changes envisaged by the project are changes in the rules of the Tax Order. Unfavourable for taxpayers are among others. changes to the general clause, the interpretation of individual, the additional tax liability. The project also introduces a reporting obligation, which is to be imposed on tax advisers, lawyers, legal advisers and other entities. This is the implementation of the Legislative Disclosure Rules Directive, but here the projector has also gone further than the Directive. Of course, there is a problem here in relation to the mystery guaranteed by the rules applicable to certain professions. Tax advisors and legal counsels are very negative about these solutions. It is worth noting that they were not consulted with them. The Minister said earlier that they were, but it turned out that they were only consulted assumptions, not solutions. Many of the project's solutions are very low, especially in language. There are problems with understanding many regulations, and about how this project was designed from the language side, it is best to say that our Legislative Office said that a few hundred commas will have to be inserted there - several hundred, please. In the explanatory memorandum to the project we read that its aim is to simplify tax law. I think he certainly doesn't simplify tax law. Given that we are dealing with a very low linguistic and technical quality of the project, that there was no consultation that from the point of view of the ATAD Directive there is no need to adopt it today, we are in favour of this - I propose - that the part that concerns ordination, [[♪ The bell ♪]] As requested by tax advisors and other professional groups, it was excluded from this project and was perfected as a separate project. Thank you very much.

This bill, among others. introduces a new entitlement to the Head of National Tax Administration, allowing for the repeal of individual interpretations issued if the facts or future events described therein are subject to the conditions set out in Article. 14b § 5b Tax Order. In addition, the Act introduces a new regulation on the establishment of an additional tax liability, so-called. sanctions, in case of a decision to apply the double taxation avoidance clause, which may also indirectly result in additional burdens for businesses, but will not eliminate aggressive tax optimization. In addition, as regards issues related to interpretations, so-called. security opinions have also been issued only two. And now you want to force entrepreneurs to replace individual interpretations with protective opinions, with an individual interpretation being a fee of 40 PLN, while a protective opinion is 20 000 PLN. zł. Once again, at the expense of the Polish entrepreneur, instead of improving tax law, simplifying tax law, you are introducing further regulations which are significantly confusing this tax law. In addition, you are in breach of the obligation of professional secrecy to report tax schemes. You introduce this duty to experts, counselors, lawyers. Which of the entrepreneurs will now come to the legal counsel, the lawyer or the expert asking for tax advice? No. However, the State does not distinguish between aggressive tax optimization and correct application of tax rules in its law. In your opinion, only lawyers, lawyers or tax advisors act as part of aggressive tax optimization, which is completely untrue. And the tax, which is a new additional charge, the exit tax, so-called. exit tax. Once again, there is a situation where once again you do not distinguish between the situation of tax optimization and the choice by the trader of more favourable tax conditions. In addition, you are introducing, using the concept of implementing the EU directive, adverse solutions beyond this directive concerning the taxation of exit tax of individuals, and you want to introduce these negative solutions for Polish entrepreneurs into our legal system a year earlier than required by the directive. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u17	Thank you, sir. I invite Mr Krzysztof Paszyk, a club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u18	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I would like to present the position of the club concerning the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on income tax on individuals, the Act on corporate income tax, the Act on Tax Ordination and some other laws contained in the Seym printing no. 2860. There are no revolutionary changes after the committee's work on this project. The most important solutions for simplifying tax law according to this project are the revision of transfer pricing rules, as well as the reduction of bureaucratic and administrative burdens for entrepreneurs, in particular small and medium-sized, preferential taxation of income generated by intellectual property rights, the introduction of specific arrangements for economic operators to purchase sets of claims, as well as the introduction of a concession for cooperative banks making contributions to the Institutional Protection System, the introduction of an alternative way of taxing eurobonds and the introduction of separate rules on the taxation of virtual currency income. The proposed amendments also include the introduction of: mandatory disclosure of tax schemes, which is a partial adaptation to the EU Directive of 25 May 2018 - this solution is intended to allow a precise and effective fight against aggressive tax optimization - changes in withholding tax under both income tax laws, changes in the general clause against tax avoidance. In addition, the project provides for changes concerning the introduction in the catalogue of corporate tax exemptions of the flat-rate income tax exemption paid by the National Bank of Poland in connection with the statutory task which the central bank carries out in the public interest under the Act on NBP in the field of foreign reserve management. In addition, the proposal extends the scope of the exemption for investment funds. As regards transfer pricing developments, it is proposed to reduce documentation obligations. There is an increase in the documentary thresholds, after which the obligation to draw up transfer pricing documentation arises. This solution will significantly reduce obligations for most taxpayers, in particular micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. The mechanism for setting thresholds will also change, which will be much easier to apply. The deadlines have also been extended. The time limits for submitting a statement on the preparation of local transfer pricing documentation and the submission of transfer pricing information were extended from 3 to 9 months after the end of the tax year. In turn, the deadline for drawing up the group transfer pricing documentation will be even longer and will be 12 months after the end of the tax year. The project also includes facilitations and exemptions for documentation. This is the exemption from the obligation to prepare transfer pricing documentation for transactions between national entities fulfilling certain conditions. In addition, it was possible to fulfil the obligation to have group transfer pricing documentation by using directly the documentation drawn up by another group entity, including in English for this purpose. As a result, taxpayers who receive such documentation from a group will not be obliged to draw it up themselves. There could still be a lot of solutions here, while I focused on important ones. They actually go hand in hand with these demands that entrepreneurs raise, and I think it's worth working on the project, for which we as a parliamentary club are in favour. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u32	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for these questions, I will try to address them, to some I may have to answer in writing. Mr Kobylinski's first question on the 5% CIT we are introducing with regard to intellectual property. This is a package that contains solutions called IP Box. Isn't it worth reducing taxes hard instead of reducing taxes in these specific areas? In our tax policy, we assume that we are lowering taxes in those areas where it is needed, and we see this need for intellectual property, because this is something that is dragging the economy along. That's why we want there to be a directional relief. It is in line with the OECD guidelines, in line with the assumptions set out by the European Commission, other countries have such reliefs, too, so we have decided to introduce them. On the other hand, in another law, but within the same package, we present a 90% CIT tax for small CIT taxpayers, i.e. for companies that do not exceed 1200 000. Euro turnover per year. This is another proposal to give a little support to small companies that are at such a stage of development that it would be good for this stage to survive and to develop. That's why we want to, and that's why we're putting down the stakes. And we think that these are solutions that will positively affect the Polish economy. Mr Jerzy Meysztowicz asked about the ombudsman's opinion. As far as I recall, his opinion was not influenced, he did not exercise the right to make an opinion within this legislative process, but the opinion was made by various stakeholders, I think, first of all, entrepreneurs. Many confederations of entrepreneurs, business organisations presented their opinions and we took them into account in part. Mrs Zofia Czernow asked if she was concerned that the law had to be understood. Of course this is true, this is the way it should be, and I would like to point out that a very large part of the issues that we are standardizing are technical issues that concern professionals and professionals. And second question: Are we preparing proposals for bringing these rules closer to taxpayers? Yes, I promise that to all tax provisions, to all solutions that will enter into force from 1 January, to the extent that we are introducing new solutions on the tax base, rates or method of payment, we will prepare special tax explanations provided for in the Tax Order. Applying them will protect taxpayers from the negative consequences. We intend to do this in cooperation with business organisations and among others. A spokesperson for business rights. It seems to me that this is the role such an institution as the institution of the Ombudsman should play in the process of creating or applying the law. I therefore answer Mr Piotr Pyzik's question as to whether we are providing a role for the Ombudsman. We will certainly issue tax explanations to these laws and we will certainly benefit from the experience of the ombudsman. As regards the implementing rules, those implementing rules which are mandatory will be published on 1 January 2019. Janusz Cichoń asked how the proposal for changes to the Tax Order, which we are now looking at, is to work on the new Tax Order. I am a member of the codification committee for the Ministry of Finance and our cooperation with the codification committee is very good. This is a reliable committee of experts who doesn't have a political cut, but has an expert instinct to prepare a good project. And this good design is on the table, it's being processed. It is currently being consulted in the Joint Government and Territorial Government Commission. We agreed with the Codification Commission that this project will be enriched with solutions that we are introducing on an ongoing basis into the existing provisions of the Tax Order. I understand that maybe not all new solutions are in line with the assumptions of the new Tax Order, but it must be remembered that the assumptions of the new Tax Order, if I remember correctly, were prepared in 2014 - at least some of them - were not yet there. She was, for example. The tax evasion clause, but it's been introduced, it's already in the new ordination. Part of these solutions, such as those contained in the Tax Schema Information Exchange Directive, have simply not yet been present, have appeared in the OECD report, BEPS, or Base Erosion Profit Shifting. As I recall, it was 2012. [[Exhibitions in the O.R.]] 2015? I'm sorry, I'm not sure of a date. But when we implement these solutions, we are basing ourselves on these OECD guidelines. On the part of Professor. Nykiela had earlier argued that we were extending the directives. We do not extend directives, we introduce directives as they are, but we introduce national solutions that are similar to those contained in directives that we consider necessary to make the system complete. This is because we cannot put in place a solution that concerns legal persons when we do not introduce a solution that concerns natural persons. [[They're going beyond the directive.]] In my opinion, they do not go beyond the Directive, because we implement the way the Directive wants for legal persons, but our own solutions, for example. concerning reporting of tax schemes, Professor, we have begun to introduce, i.e. we started talking about it in 2017. And then there was a pre-consultation on the assumptions regarding the introduction of the mandate disclosure. At that time we also discussed professional secrecy with representatives of regulated professions who presented their proposals. We have softened the original project, we have made assumptions about how we want to introduce it, and they have said that we should respect professional secrecy more. We have taken this into account in this respect, that we provide for the possibility that reporting will be part of the tasks of the beneficiary, i.e. implementing an optimization scheme, not to an advisor, a lawyer, a legal adviser or anyone who provides services in the context of regulated professions. So I declare that we have implemented the directive to the extent that it provides that it needs to be implemented, while at the same time we introduce our national solutions as a sovereign state which protects the interests of the Polish Treasury. That is why we are introducing them, not because there is a directive. We are not here to implement directives only in terms of what the directive provides for, because the directive acts cross-borderly, it protects - e.g. in the framework of the exchange of tax information, the interests of the treasury of other EU Member States. We Reporting and exchanging schematics, inform that e.g. could lose the German state or the Luxembourg state, let's assume. In return, however, we want to obtain the same care for the interests of the Polish Treasury in Luxembourg and that they inform us that there is something on the matter that the Polish Treasury may lose something. This falls within the framework of the exchange of tax information on cross-border schemes, and this is regulated by the Directive, because only in this respect can the Directive regulate this. The Directive cannot regulate the reporting of national schemes because it is an internal matter of each country and not a cross-border one, so it goes beyond the powers of the European Union, which are exercised on a subsidiarity basis. As far as we are talking about national schemes, based on the OECD's BEPS 12 report, we are implementing disclosure mandates as part of national optimisation schemes. So that I disagree, I simply cannot agree with the argument that we are extending the directive that we are... [[Speech Off the Microphone]] Okay, if that's okay. [[There are no individuals.]] We introduce a reporting scheme and an exit tax tax on individuals because it is in line with the interest of the Polish Treasury. The directive only talks about exit tax on companies, because once again the European Union... [[About the subjects...]] ...does not have the authority to regulate the internal affairs of a given country with regard to individuals, because not every natural person is an entrepreneur. The Union has only regulated this issue when someone is clearly an entrepreneur when he is a legal person who is an entrepreneur... [[Thirty-fifth tax...]] ...and then the Union has the right to regulate cross-border issues that concern the internal market in an economic sense. In this respect, the Union has regulated this and we are regulating it to the extent that it concerns the protection of the Treasury under the national order. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u163	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Wiesław Janczyk, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u164	Thank you very much. At the moment I am acting in a new role and I have the honour and pleasure to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the proposal to amend the Act on Compulsory Insurance, Insurance Guarantee Fund and the Polish Office of Communication Insurances. In fact, the proposal aims to align the current amounts of liability insurance for motor vehicle holders with the regulation adopted in Directive 2009/103/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 September 2009 on insurance against such liability. This directive is commonly referred to as the Communication Directive. This provision provides that, every 5 years or at the end of the transitional period, the amounts of guarantee amounts shall be reviewed taking into account the index of consumer prices, so-called. HICP. Poland benefited from a transitional period to adjust the minimum guarantee amounts, which lasted until 11 June 2012. Therefore, the minimum guarantee amounts will be increased by the percentage change indicated by the HICP for a period of 5 years by the end of the transitional period and rounded to 10 000. Euros. For the period from June 2012 to June 2017, this indicator increased by 4.14%. Thus, the minimum guarantee amount will not be less than 5210 thousand. the euro for one event. Currently, this sum amounts to EUR 5 million in the event of personal injury. The guarantee sum will increase in the event of damage to the property to 1050 thousand. Euros. It is currently EUR 1 million. These changes will apply in the same way to OC insurance for farmers. The increase in the minimum guarantee amounts will have little effect on the increase in the contribution and, consequently, on the increase in compensation paid to victims and victims of accidents. The proposed law is to enter into force on 31 December this year. The Law and Justice Club supports these regulations. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The project submitted concerns an increase in the minimum guarantee amounts in compulsory insurance of motor vehicle holders for damage caused by the use of these vehicles and in compulsory insurance of the civil liability of farmers for holding an agricultural holding. In addition, the draft contains proposals to clarify the rules on the party liable to pay compensation to the injured party in the event of a possible dispute between the Insurance Guarantee Fund and the insurance undertaking. The need for this amendment arises from the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 September 2009 on insurance against civil liability arising from the use of motor vehicles and the enforcement of the obligation to cover such liability. On the basis of the article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of that Directive every 5 years from 11 June 2005 or at the end of the transitional period until 11 June 2012 at the latest, the amounts of the guarantee amounts referred to in Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 shall be reviewed taking into account the index of consumer prices. The Republic of Poland has benefited from a transitional period to adjust the minimum guarantee amounts until 11 June 2012 and as a result of the review which took place, new guarantee amounts have been identified which are the subject of this project. In addition, the amendments proposed in the draft law aim to clarify the applicable rules in the event of a possible dispute between the Insurance Guarantee Fund and the insurance undertaking concerning the determination of which one is obliged to pay the benefit to the injured person. As proposed in the event of any dispute between the above mentioned finding which of them is obliged to pay compensation to the injured party, such payment shall be made by the insurance undertaking. According to the proposal, if, after payment by the insurance undertaking, the liability of the Insurance Guarantee Fund is established in whole or in part, the fund will be obliged to reimburse the insurance undertaking for the compensation paid or part of it and the costs incurred. Of course, ladies and gentlemen, this legislative proposal, on the one hand, concerns only the increase of the guarantee amounts, and on the other hand, it is impossible to escape the question of how this regulation will affect the determination of insurance premiums. In the draft law in the explanatory memorandum and in the OSR we find that this impact will be scarce in the short term, while it is interesting - and here I would ask the Ministry for more detailed explanations - that is the wording that concerns the longer term. According to the opinion presented by the Insurance Board by the Polish Insurance Association, the increase in the guarantee amounts is expected to increase. This in the long term may entail higher costs for the insurance undertaking, which will mean an increase, which is significant even, as shown by the position presented, insurance premiums. And that, of course, is disturbing. This is also worrying because a little bit of these transition periods is that for some time the contributions remain at this lower level, but it turns out that later the costs associated with the increase, the sudden increase in contributions are extremely high. The implementation of this Directive makes the conditions of insurance, the way in which the damage is eliminated and the amount of the guarantee amount identical in all the Member States of the European Union. Poland has so far benefited from a transitional period which lasted until 11 June 2012. According to this draft law, the minimum guarantee amount is increased and cannot be lower than the equivalent in PLN: in the case of damage to the person 5210 thousand. EUR per event regardless of the number of victims, currently EUR 5 million, while in the case of damage to property - 1050 000. EUR per event, currently EUR 1 million. The same changes will also be made for compulsory insurance of the civil liability of farmers for holding an agricultural holding, i.e. it will cover damage caused by the traffic of vehicles so-called. free-riders held by farmers and used in connection with holding. In conclusion, I would like to stress that the increase in minimum guarantee amounts applies to all the Member States of the European Union and is made every 5 years. Just a few more words, if you'll excuse me. The main objective of the proposed act on electronic invoicing in public contracts, works or services concessions and public-private partnerships is to implement into the Polish legal order the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on electronic invoicing in public contracts. In our country, the problem is the low use of structured electronic invoices in public procurement and economic trade. It is therefore entirely legitimate and desirable to introduce into the Polish legal system the law under consideration, which can become one of the main drivers of the development of electronic invoicing, causing the dissemination of this form of documenting transactions first in respect of the execution of public contracts and then in other areas of economic trade. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u181	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Gawron, Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice, will be the first to speak. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u182	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present my position on the Government's draft bill on electronic invoicing in public procurement, works or services concessions and public-private partnerships, print No 2888. The reason for the above law is to implement the provisions of Directive 2014/55/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on electronic invoicing in public procurement into the Polish legal order. The main objective of the directive is to remove or reduce barriers to cross-border trade resulting from the coexistence of a number of legal requirements and technical standards for electronic invoicing. This objective is to be achieved by obliging public administrations at all levels to accept invoices in electronic form in accordance with the relevant European standard, which are issued by economic operators in connection with the implementation of contracts concluded in the field of public procurement and concessions. Given the nature of the proposed amendments, as well as the variety of areas to regulate the proposed provisions, the most appropriate solution is to include these changes in a separate law. The draft law provides for the regulation of the public procurement process, i.e. the phase from the conclusion of the contract for the award of contracts until their execution, receipt and issue of an invoice. An additional objective is to introduce the possibility of sending structured electronic invoices between IT systems in public procurement as one of the areas of invoicing. Contracting authorities' obligation to adopt, inter alia, e-invoices in an appropriate format, in line with the European standard, will enable the implementation of this Directive and will enable the technical and technological solutions offered in trade to be organised. It should also be pointed out that the invoice is rarely a stand-alone document that occurs in economic trade. And that's what happens. accounting note, corrective note, contract, order, delivery confirmation, transfer-receiver protocol. All these documents are substantially related to the invoice. The proposed law will not regulate matters or affect matters contained in civil law, but will only impose certain obligations on contracting entities, contractors in the performance of public contracts to settle them in accordance with the law on value added tax. In our country, no rules have yet been introduced to require the use of such formalised electronic invoices in the public procurement system. As examples of other countries show, they are one of the main drivers of the development of electronic invoicing. Given the circumstances presented, the Law and Justice Club supports the proposed solutions and, after considering any amendments tabled, we do not propose amendments, we will vote in favour of this law. Thank you very much.

This result is one of the lowest places in Europe, it is about getting used to the paper version of documents because of the fear of losing data from electronic media, imprecise legislation, the prospect of not accepting invoices by the tax office or simply of unwillingness to use the Internet. And although all available research confirms the high awareness of Poles about the benefits of using electronic documents, the use of e-invoices is still very low. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u237	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Mrs Anna Czech, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u238	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws, print No 2919. This draft amending act is intended to amend the article. 7 Act of 18 March 2016 amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. Firstly, this concerns the amount of funding for medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses or medical devices covered by the 75+ programme foreseen for 2018. The second amendment is to amend the principle of adjusting the correction mechanism where it concerns a change in the risk of exceeding the limits fixed for the subsequent years of implementation of the 75+ programme. As the rapporteur said, the expenditure limit for 2018 is 643 300 000. PLN and in the 10-year funding period it will amount to 8 274 600 000. zł. In the Act we change, increase these values by PLN 50 million. This is due to the need for an extended range of funding, because the 75+ programme is simply very well implemented and there is a need for an increase in funding. This need to increase funding directly translates into the health situation of older people and indirectly into the economic situation. This is indeed part of the implementation of the State's commitment to the elderly. According to Art. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Older people's constitutions should be surrounded by the particular concern of the state in the field of health protection. This is how public authorities try to implement this provision of the Constitution. The second amendment modifies the corrective mechanism governed by Article. Paragraph 7 Paragraph 4 (2) of the amended Act. It provided for a recovery mechanism to amend the list and now another solution needs to be implemented. Where any of the four quarters of a given year exceed the equivalent of 25% the expected limit for this year, this mechanism should be increased to 35%, because the implementation of this programme is not strictly 1/12/1/12: in one month the funding amounts are higher and in another month the funding will be lower, so this will be more favourable. It shall also ensure that the list can be properly monitored and limited, i.e. expenditure of the appropriations as provided for in the year in question. In the light of the above submissions, the Law and Justice Club supports the solutions proposed in this Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u242	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The project has basically three objectives. The second objective is to amend the overall maximum limit of appropriations for this purpose for the period 2016 to 2025. The third objective is to amend the principle of the corrective mechanism, which is applied by the Minister of Health in the event of a risk of exceeding the limits set for the implementation of the 75+ programme. The explanatory memorandum of the bill states that it is necessary to adapt the funding limit to extend the scope of the 75+ programme, since the increase in revenue from the tax sealing system will allow for an extension of the list of medicines available to the beneficiaries of the 75+ programme, which in turn requires an increase in the envelope for its operation. There's no explanation for the mode... ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u265	Thank you, sir. As there are no questions to this item, the discussion is closed. We will proceed to the vote on the draft resolution in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 10 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Bank Guarantee Fund Act, the deposit guarantee scheme and the compulsory restructuring and some other laws (print No 2877). I invite the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Finance of Mr Piotr Nowak to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u266	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! By presenting you with a draft law amending the Bank Guarantee Fund Act, the deposit guarantee scheme and the compulsory restructuring system and some other laws, I would like to briefly present the context of the project and the solutions proposed therein, and then point out the most important changes that this project brings to the existing legal order. In 2014, the Resolution Directive for credit institutions and investment firms entered into force, i.e. the BRR Directive, which was the answer to the main problem identified in connection with the recent financial crisis. Afraid of the negative effects of the bankruptcy of financial institutions on the financial system and the economy as a whole, many countries around the world have decided to rescue insolvent, vulnerable entities using public funds. In view of the huge scale of the costs of these interventions for state budgets and thus for society, it was necessary to develop a new approach in which public authorities would have the necessary tools and powers to restructure such institutions in the event of financial institutions' problems, and the costs of action would be borne primarily by the risk institutions themselves and the owners and creditors of those institutions and not by taxpayers. Such solutions were contained in the BRR Directive, which was implemented in the Polish legal order under the Act of 10 June 2016 on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the Deposit Insurance Scheme and the Forced Restructuring. By virtue of this Law, BFG, which has so far been primarily responsible for the protection of depositors and merely supporting restructuring processes carried out in certain entities, has also become an authority for forced restructuring and has therefore been entrusted with new, additional powers related to forced restructuring activities. Together with the BRR Directive, the 2016 BFG Act also implemented the Deposit Guarantee Schemes Directive, so-called. DGS Directive aimed at strengthening the protection of depositors and greater harmonisation in Member States of solutions already existing in this area. In order to enable the compulsory restructuring process to be carried out, the BRR Directive introduced an obligation for credit institutions and investment firms to comply with the minimum own funds requirement and so-called "own funds requirement." eligible liabilities, i.e. liabilities to be remitted counter to the ranking of creditors in insolvency proceedings for the purpose of recapitalisation of the institution, commonly called. bail-in. In practice, however, these regulations have proved inadequately precise and have created uncertainties and limitations for the efficiency of this process. Inclusion in the bail-in process the category of claims of different types of instruments, i.e. subject to and not subject to bail-in, generated a risk of the occurrence of claims against creditors that would be treated worse than in the standard insolvency procedure. Against this background, in the absence of a uniform approach at EU level, some countries have changed their rules on the hierarchy of unsecured senior debt under national insolvency laws, leading to disharmonisation at EU level and uncertainty for both issuing institutions and investors. The experience of the Italian banks has shown that, in some cases, the lack of sufficient eligible liabilities to MREL prevents this restructuring process from being carried out, so-called. resolution. The answer to these problems was the Directive adopted at the end of 2017 on the degree of preference of unsecured debt instruments in the hierarchy of claims in insolvency proceedings, so-called. Directive BCH amending inter alia BRR Directive. The BCH Directive introduced a new category of debt claims, so-called debt claims. non-privileged debt, and identified the place of that debt in the hierarchy of settlement of claims under insolvency proceedings. The emergence of a new category with the possibility of issuing new types of debt instruments is intended to facilitate institutions' compliance with the MREL obligation and thus improve the resolution process for the use of the bail-in instrument. It should be stressed that the proposed law aims primarily at the timely fulfilment of national adjustment obligations in the form of implementation of the above mentioned provisions. Directive by 29 December this year. by amending the Law of 28 February 2003 on insolvency law. Therefore, a new category of non-privileged debt is introduced, so-called. a minor non-privileged debt, which in the insolvency law hierarchy will be in category six - it is currently category nine - so that the current categories from the sixth will be shifted accordingly. The new debt group will constitute bonds as well as other negotiable debt instruments, provided that they fulfil a number of conditions laid down in the proposed legislation. These conditions are as follows: the term for which the bonds are issued shall be at least 1 year, the contract or the terms of issue shall clearly specify the category of settlement of claims under bankruptcy law, the instruments issued shall not result from derivatives or structured financial products. In addition, an important condition is among others. that the nominal value of a single bond cannot be less than 400 000. zł or the equivalent of that amount expressed in another currency. This is aimed at securing non-professional investors who should not purchase risky instruments as an alternative to deposits. The situation on the Polish financial market is stable and so far there has been no need for forced restructuring, the initiation of which is subject to compliance with the conditions laid down in the provisions of the Law on BFG. This does not change the fact that the continuous development of the financial market and the phenomena observed therein, potential risks, constraints and opportunities determine the need to improve functioning solutions. Also in the case of solutions adopted in the Law on BFG over 2 years of application of the provisions of this Law, some difficulties or interpretation doubts have been identified. In addition, the need to supplement or refine existing legislation in certain areas has been recognised, including taking into account the European Commission's observations in the framework of the review of the transposition of EU legislation. In the light of the above, it is worth pointing out that the proposed law provides for the systematic exclusion of mortgage banks from the provisions on deposit guarantee schemes and related obligations and charges for information, financial, administrative and other purposes, as mortgage banks they do not accept deposits, provide guarantee protection to the BFG for the funds collected in the bank accounts for the advice of parents working at schools for which such accounts may be maintained under educational law - so far the rules have not allowed such protection; extend the options for managing the BFG's financial resources in the investment area by introducing the possibility for BFG to place free funds in the deposit with the Minister of Finance. Another important change is to allow the use of the compulsory restructuring instrument so-called. the bridge institution in the restructuring of the merging bank and the introduction of an improvement enabling the establishment and start-up of an accelerated bridge institution in the event of a crisis in the entity. In the context of the changes that have been made to reduce legal obligations, it has been proposed, inter alia.: to reduce certain legal and operational constraints related to the use of compulsory restructuring instruments and the banking activities of the Guarantee Fund, for example: in the restructuring of the pool bank by means of a bridge institution instrument and in the process of consulting potential buyers of restructuring banks and cash; introducing the possibility for the Bank's Guarantee Fund to limit the implementation by smaller banks for which it is not planned to apply compulsory restructuring instruments, certain information obligations on the fund. High Izbo! In the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Poland, the adoption of the proposed law should increase the security of the banking sector in Poland. In the long term, the proposed solutions will increase the security and stability of the financial system, thereby reducing the risk of public funds being involved in rescuing those who are threatened with bankruptcy in the future. Therefore, on behalf of the Government, I ask the High House to support this proposal. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u267	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I invite you to the speaker of Mr Jan Szewczak, Law and Justice. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u268	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, the reason for all this was the already famous 2008 crisis, when it was realized that, however, Nowak rules, i.e. that when a financial institution comes to bankruptcy, the costs of it are simply borne by society and customers. Then they began to look for new solutions. There are different assessments as to whether those proposed in these EU directives are actually 100 per cent effective, but it is certainly some form of search for an impact on these activities, on public finances, so that they are not burdened with such events, not to mention customers, depositaries. Hence these ideas for greater participation of bondholders, etc. However, this is not entirely certain either, because the situation mentioned here by the Italian Minister shows that there are serious problems in the bond area, too, which may affect financial stability in the state. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u273	Thank you, sir. I invite you to present your position on behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the European Union of Democrats, Mrs Genowefa Tokarska.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u274	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position towards the government's draft law amending the Bank Guarantee Fund Act, the deposit guarantee system and the forced restructuring system and some other laws, print no. 2877. The draft law is the implementation of Directive 2017/2399 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 December 2017 amending Directive 2014/59/EU, which established a framework for the recovery and resolution of credit institutions and investment firms, and amending Regulations of the European Parliament and of the Council. It's so-called. a BRR directive aimed at increasing the efficiency of the instrument for redemption or conversion of liabilities. The proposal harmonises terminology, refines rules and, above all, introduces substantive changes, e.g. exempts mortgage banks from the deposit guarantee scheme - it is simple because mortgage banks does not accept deposits - improves the restructuring of the association bank by creating a bridge institution, corrects the provisions of the Banking Guarantee Fund for the restructuring of cooperative savings banks, guarantees cover bank accounts of parents' councils at schools, reduces certain restrictions on compulsory restructuring and corrects the implementation of the article. 45 and Article 45 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 55 BRRs which offer the possibility to request additional conditions for own funds and eligible liabilities. In general, the proposal amends eight laws of Polish law by implementing the directive. The project is very extensive in its content, detailed, written not very easy language. Due to the harmonisation of EU law, the commitment to implement EU directives, the Polish People's Party Club - the Union of European Democrats is in favour of directing the project to work in committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u276	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Excuse me, I was at the public finance committee meeting, which is why I'm late. I would like to present a print project No. 2904, i.e. draft law amending the act on tax on goods and services. This is a project which partially implements two directives: Directive 2016/1065 of June 2016 concerning vouchers for goods and services and Directive 2017/2455 of December 2017 concerning value added tax for the provision of distance sales, distance sales of goods and services. Member States are required to implement both Directives by 31 December 2018 and to apply those provisions from 1 January 2019. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The amendments resulting from the implementation of Directive 2016/1065, hereinafter referred to as the voucher or voucher directive, aim at simplifying, modernising and harmonising the VAT rules applicable to vouchers for goods or services. This problem has not yet been regulated at EU level and the directive we are talking about harmonising the rules for using two types of vouchers. The first type are single-purpose vouchers, i.e. one-purpose vouchers, and the second-purpose vouchers are multiple-purpose vouchers. As far as the first type is concerned, we have a voucher of one purpose which will be taxed at the time of its issue, i.e. the transfer of that voucher will be treated as if the service had been performed or the goods were delivered. On the other hand, for vouchers of different destinies, so-called. Multipurpose vouchers, they will cover different goods, you will be able to purchase different goods for them, different services and therefore the use of such vouchers will be treated as if the payment had taken place, i.e. the transfer of this voucher will not result in a duty on goods and services tax. Such vouchers are quite often used in economic practice, e.g. it may happen that the employer issues such vouchers to employees, for example. They could have dinner at work at break. The aim is to harmonise and make it a little easier to use these facilities. As regards the second Directive on the provision of services and the sale of goods at a distance, our implementation involves electronic, telecommunications and broadcasting services provided to consumers from other Member States. Some minor simplification is introduced, which will involve the provision of services, the supply of such electronic services at a distance, i.e. if. someone provides streaming or sharing services via websites, then he will not be required to pay VAT in the country where the service is provided, only in accordance with the simplification introduced will he be able - if he does not exceed the amount of 10 000. The euro, which in our implementation represents a threshold of 42 000. zł - pay this VAT in our country. Therefore, he will not have to register in the country where he provides services. At the moment there is already such a convenience, which is referred to from English as mini they stop shop, i.e. one which causes that someone who provides a distance service in another country can, instead of registering in that other country, using this mini they stop shop, here in Poland, via the website to pay a tax which is due in another country where they provide services. In fact, this facility, which we are proposing at the moment, concerns the introduction of this threshold of 42 000. zł. The proposed law is to enter into force on 1 January 2019, which is consistent with the date set out in thebon Directive and in the Directive on the sale of services and goods at a distance. The proposed law is in agreement with the law of the European Union. In presenting the above, I kindly ask the High House to refer this proposal to further parliamentary work. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u278	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The draft Law amending the Law on VAT on goods and services, print No 2904, provides for amendments to the Act of 11 March 2004 on tax on goods and services intended to implement Directive 2016/1065 of 27 June 2016 and Directive 2017/2455 of 5 December 2017. The purpose of this first Directive, i.e. 2016/1065, is to simplify, modernise and harmonise the VAT rules applicable to vouchers for goods or services. Currently, in the absence of regulation at EU level and in the absence of a number of case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union, Member States apply divergent rules on the taxation of vouchers for goods or services. In order to ensure a uniform approach, to remain consistent with the principles of general consumer tax and to avoid inconsistencies and distortions of competition, double taxation or non-taxation and to reduce the risk of tax avoidance, it has become necessary to lay down specific rules applicable to the taxation of vouchers for VAT goods or services. The introduction of rules specifying the taxation of vouchers is also important in view of the rules on the place of supply of telecommunications services, broadcasting services, electronic services applicable from 1 January 2015. It is necessary to ensure that there is no inconsistencies with vouchers delivered between Member States. Directive 2016/1065 introduces the definition of vouchers for goods or services and distinguishes between their types and regulates the rules on taxation of transactions using vouchers as well as the taxable base for vouchers of different uses. That Directive refers to a voucher for goods or services defined as an instrument with which it is obliged to accept it as remuneration or part of the remuneration for the supply of goods or services and for which the goods or services to be supplied or performed are indicated in the instrument itself or in the related documentation. In practice, vouchers will acquire more and more features that characterize payment instruments. With such instruments, it is necessary to check individually whether the definition of the voucher is met. When using a voucher depending on its type of VAT it will be charged at the time of transfer of the voucher or at the time of use of the voucher. VAT charged related to the voucher, e.g. for the costs incurred in the design or printing of the voucher, it may therefore, as a general rule, be eligible for deduction by the issuer of the voucher on a general basis relating to VAT. The provisions of the Directive do not concern situations where a voucher of different uses is not executed by the final consumer within the period of validity of the voucher and the remuneration received for such voucher is retained by the seller. There are vouchers of one destiny and vouchers of different destinies. I'm not going to talk about them anymore, the Minister said it. I also want to talk about Directive 2017/2455. Provisions in Article. 1 of this Directive is primarily aimed at reducing the costs and nuisance of VAT accounting for telecommunications, broadcasting and electronic services. In short, I would just like to say that the proposed laws have a major impact on micro-entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized enterprises, especially in terms of regulating the taxation of goods and services vouchers on goods and services, which will guarantee taxpayers legal certainty and a uniform approach to VAT rules. This is relevant when it comes to changing the qualification of certain prepaid cards. [[♪ The bell ♪]] It was also mentioned by the Minister. I just want to say one more thing that the rules are due to enter from 1 January 2019. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u308	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present a proposal for a draft Print Act No 2901, i.e. a draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act. In short, it is a project that introduces a thermomodernization relief. This is linked to the acceleration of the process of improving air quality. We want to help this process through tax instruments. This is one of the priorities of government policy. An important action to improve air quality in Poland is the thermomodernization of single-family residential buildings. The project that I am presenting provides for facilitations in this respect. I must also mention that, in order to carry out this action, concerning clean air, a priority programme has been launched, under which owners and co-owners of single-family housing will be able to benefit from funding from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the Regional Environmental and Water Management Funds. This is a fund dedicated to the implementation of thermomodernization projects. The proposed bill complements the available forms of thermomodernisation support granted under the Clean Air programme. It contains a tax incentive for persons who own single-family residential buildings, which will carry out the thermomodernization of these buildings from their own resources. This is a thermomodernization relief. In addition, the project proposes a exemption from income tax for subsidies granted under the "Clean Air' programme, whether from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management, or from voivodship environmental and water management funds. The basic solution envisaged by this project is the relief to individuals' income tax taxpayers, both those who pay the tax on a normal, progressive basis, at a linear rate of 19% as well as those who pay a lump sum on registered revenues. The proposed solution consists of the possibility of deduction from income or revenue, depending on the type of tax, of expenditure relating to the implementation of thermomodernization projects in single-family housing. The deduction from revenue, or from revenue, will be 23% expenditure incurred for the implementation of the project. The maximum deduction limit will be 53 000. zł. The possibility of benefiting from the relief will be conditional upon the completion of the thermomodernisation project in the period of 3 years from the end of the year in which the first expenditure on the project will be incurred. At the same time, the proposal proposes to establish, on the basis of the Regulation, a list of types of building materials, equipment and services covered by the proposed relief. This will make it easier for taxpayers to benefit from this relief - they will simply know the extent of possible deductions. The second important element of the project is the exemption from subsidy tax that taxpayers can obtain from the Clean Air programme. Under the current tax-free legislation, subsidies are received from the state budget or from the budgets of local government units. The solution that we propose extends this exemption also to grants granted under the "Clean Air' programme, which will be financed by the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the relevant voivodship environmental and water management funds. Such grants will be exempted. We hope that this will reduce emissions, reduce energy intensity of single-family homes and reduce atmospheric pollution. Having put it this way, I would like to ask the High House to direct this project to further parliamentary work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The relief consists of deduction from revenue, which is very important, of the expenditure incurred on thermomodernisation. The conditions for benefiting from the tax credit are as follows. On income, 23% can be deducted expenditure incurred, except that the maximum amount of the allowance may not exceed a total of 53 000. zł, the time limit is 3 years for one project and, most importantly, the expenditure will be documented by VAT invoices issued only by taxable persons for goods and services not benefiting from exemption from this tax and the relief is not entitled to taxable persons for expenditure financed by the national environmental fund, the provincial fund or have been reimbursed in any form whatsoever, that is obvious. But this project raises serious doubts and reservations. Why? First of all, why is the project limited to single-family construction? And multi-family construction, including housing communities, including small housing communities, where people often live with low incomes, and housing is often heated with furnaces, where coal is often burned with bad quality coal, Mr. President, I'm sorry? Multi-family construction is also a source of significant emissions. It cannot be ignored in this bill. The second reservation concerns the financing of the project. Here is a very cunning financing system. The explanatory memorandum states how this government will help the owners of the buildings to fight smog. Unfortunately, it's missing the truth. This is directly due to the justification of the project. The government will actually earn PLN 960 million on this proposal. Who's gonna pay? Of course, self-government. The government will receive additional VAT revenue for this project, close to PLN 3 billion, which will cover losses in the PIT of the state budget, and the net state budget will receive a surplus of 960 million. And the losses in local government income will amount to almost PLN 2 billion and no one will cover it. The Act does not provide for any compensation for local authorities. Why? - I'm asking. It's against the constitution. I do not know whether the government knows that local governments have systematically financed the expenditure of their inhabitants from their own budgets for the fight against smog activities, including the exchange of furnaces, the cost of connection to heating networks, etc. Why does the government want to finance their ideas, not out of their pockets, but out of the pockets of local governments? Is it okay to make money on this project and to shift costs to others while misleading public opinion, speaking of great support? We proceed with the examination of item 14. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on National Tax Administration and some other laws (print No 2905). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr. Paweł Cybulski to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u332	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The primary objective of the amendments introduced in the draft law on the amendment of the National Tax Administration Act and some other laws, Sejm No. 2905, is to combat the grey zone and tax fraud due to the smoother operation of the National Tax Administration. This effect is to be achieved by improving the organisation of the National Tax Administration, providing greater access to the information necessary for carrying out its statutory tasks, and by providing tools for more effective execution of customs-treasure checks. In order to ensure the smooth and efficient performance of the tasks of the National Tax Administration in accordance with the proposed solutions, the Minister of Finance, which is the body of the National Tax Administration, will be equipped with instruments to strengthen the coordination of tasks carried out by the National Tax Administration, including the Customs and Treasury Service. This will also enable the Finance Minister to strengthen the minister's management role in the process of analysing and implementing solutions to improve the state's treasury, which will contribute to the smooth collection of state revenue by this apparatus. Thus, the proposed amendments to the law are intended to improve revenue collection. They also respond to the experience gained so far from the application of the provisions of the National Tax Administration Act. It should be stressed that the new instruments respond to the changing environment of the tax services and to the new opportunities offered by the technology and the operation of the tax services on the data at their disposal. This is also intended to contribute to reducing the administrative burden on taxpayers. In order to improve the flow of information in state bodies, the head of the National Tax Administration will be among others. required to provide the Finance Minister annually with a report on the activities of the National Tax Administration, which will include in particular information on the implementation of revenue from customs duties and other charges relating to the import and export of goods, the implementation of human resources and training policies in the organisational units of the National Tax Administration and the implementation of the State budget for the functioning of the National Tax Administration. With regard to access to the information necessary for carrying out the statutory tasks of the National Tax Administration, the project provides for the following changes. Firstly, the project provides for an extension of the scope of the data collected, inter alia. information on events affecting the origin or amount of non-tax fiscal claims. Secondly, the proposal provides for the possibility for national tax authorities to perform analytical activities to financial institutions with a request for information on the account holder used to operate in the shadow economy. The entitlement granted is a means to determine the data of a person who hides his data for the purpose of carrying out unnotified business taxation, obtaining non-coverable revenues in disclosed sources or from undisclosed sources, obtaining unconfirmed revenues or undisclosed revenues in submitted declarations. The mechanism is designed to equip the authorities of the National Tax Administration with the appropriate tools to seal the system and limit the grey zone. The purpose of the draft law is also to clarify and supplement the provisions concerning the arrangements for carrying out customs-credit checks, including controls concerning the possession and production of and trading of gambling machines. The bill provides for changes in the adjustment of the tax return by informing the head of the customs and treasury office carrying out the customs and treasury control of the tax return submitted by the audited correction. The head shall decide to take account of the correction of the declaration by notifying the head of the tax office. Secondly, the scope of the customs-subsistence control carried out under the permanent authorisation for control has been extended, inter alia. identification and use of excise marks and possession of gambling machines. The regulations introduced will have a positive impact on the efficiency of the national tax authorities so that they can respond to identified undesirable phenomena on an ongoing basis, and will contribute to effective supervision in the above mentioned. range. It is also envisaged to introduce regulation enabling checks to be carried out on the basis of a permanent authorisation for the production and distribution of slot machines. The proposed solution is one of the elements of a security system that allows efficient and effective elimination of the possibility of illegal slot games. Acting on the basis of a permanent authorisation shall enable action to be taken as soon as it is informed of the intention to introduce or exit slots. The creation of the regulation allows the authorities of the National Tax Administration to detect fuel from undisclosed sources from which no excise duty has been collected or no excise duty has been collected at the amount due, without the need for individual authorisation to carry out customs-credit checks each time. The practice of national tax authorities shows that fuel from undisclosed sources which do not meet the quality requirements is distributed to local petrol stations or directly to operators using such fuels. Allowing checks to be carried out on the basis of a permanent mandate for fuel quality parameters will contribute to the efficiency of carrying out customs-treasure controls in the above mentioned. areas by re-bureaucratising individual authorisation activities and increasing the response rate to undesirable phenomena. It goes on for example. the possibility of carrying out a number of inspections on a single day in the case of a number of operators. A simplified procedure for initiating checks has also been introduced and the rules for documenting the results of these checks have been amended, allowing for the rapid start-up of checks against suspicion of irregularities, in particular on the excise goods market. This will also eliminate the difficulties associated with the timely delivery of the control authority in the case of controls of petrol stations, shops, most often at the place of inspection where persons can be effectively served with the control authority. Where the seat of the entity is outside the territorial jurisdiction of the tax office chief, there may be problems with timely service of the authorisation and the cost of business travel will be generated. The draft provides for the possibility of carrying out a tax-credit control in companies forming part of a tax group which has lost the status of a taxpayer and in relation to former partners of a civil partnership, a public partnership, a limited partnership or a limited partnership in the event of the dissolution of those companies during the course of the check. The proposed provisions clarify the rules for the initiation of customs-treasure checks. The draft provides that, in the absence of a controlled representative or representative, customs and treasury control may be initiated upon presentation of a service card to the person checked or in the presence of the witness referred to, which should be a public officer who is not an employee of the Administrative Board or an officer of the Customs and Treasury Service. At the same time, a regulation has been introduced whereby, in the event of the initiation of checks on the basis of a card, the authorisation to carry out a customs-treasure check should be delivered to the audited person without undue delay. The proposed provisions aim to eliminate cases of avoidance of service of authorisation to carry out a customs-subsistence check. The project submitted in print No 2905 aims to improve the activities of the National Tax Administration, but also to reduce the burden resulting from controls on taxpayers. I ask the High House to accept the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u333	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. And I would ask Mr Łukasz Schreiber, the Law and Justice Club, to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u334	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u352	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to introduce a draft amendment to the physio therapist's profession. The Law of 25 September 2015, which entered into force on 31 May 2016, introduced regulations laying down rules for the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapist as an independent medical profession. Together with the entry into force of the Law, physiotherapists became a professional group subject to strict rules for the pursuit of this profession, which are similar to those of the profession of doctor, dentist, nurse or midwife. However, in part the powers and obligations relating to forms of pursuit of the profession of physiotherapists included those provisions which were less favourable than those mentioned above. professional groups, e.g. physiotherapists were able to pursue their profession and practice in the form of a medicinal entity, causing a number of problems and difficulties, and increasing the cost of registration of these entities. In view of the above, the Minister of Health has taken action to address issues that were not fully regulated by the Act of 25 September 2015, inter alia. through the introduction of various forms of physiotherapist profession. This draft law proposes to allow the pursuit of a physiotherapist's profession in the form of a professional practice with legal status similar to that in other medical professions. This is specified in Article. 5 Act on Medical Activity. We foresee the possibility of pursuing a physiotherapist's profession in the form of a business activity as an individual physiotherapist's practice, so-called. practice of the office, as well as proposing in this revision one-person business activity as an individual practice of the physiotherapist only at the site of the call. It provides for the possibility to pursue a physiotherapist's profession in the form of a single-person business activity as an individual practice only in a medical establishment on the basis of a contract concluded between a physiotherapist and that medical establishment, as well as the possibility to pursue a physiotherapist's profession in the form of a civil partnership, a public company or a partnership as a group practice of physiotherapists. So these solutions are exactly the same as those proposed in relation to other independent medical professions, such as I mentioned at the outset. With regard to changes in the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapist, this amendment establishes a registering body to be the National Council of Physiotherapists. It is the National Council of Physiotherapists that will keep a register of individual or group physiotherapist practices. The President of the National Council of Physiotherapists will be authorised to do so. In addition, there is a provision in the current law that requires physiotherapists to register medicinal products by the end of November of this year, which is until 30 November. As there is a large inequality in the form of activity or the pursuit of a physiotherapist's profession in relation to other medical professions, we propose in the amendment of this law that this deadline be postponed, i.e. that this registration obligation will have to be carried out within 1 April 2019. So for physiotherapists who have not yet had a registered business, and such in our opinion is about 30%, because such a number carries out an economic activity, this registration obligation will be required by 1 April 2019. In addition, the possibility for physiotherapists to register with the National Council of physiotherapists of individual or group practices set out in this amendment by physiotherapists who have so far performed a profession with registered economic activities has also been postponed by one year. These solutions meet the expectations of the physiotherapists' environment, they bear less costs, as registration of an economic activity of a medicinal entity is linked to the compliance with quite strict requirements laid down in the legislation on medicinal activities, and is also linked to a fee which is incomparably higher than the registration of an individual practice of the office or at the place of call or in the therapeutic entity. So these are rules that are more favourable to physiotherapists than those in force on the basis of the previous law. In addition, we will clarify in this amendment the issue of places of pursuit of the physiotherapist's profession. Physiotherapeutic treatments are performed not only in medicinal subjects, as so far it has been defined in the law, but also in many other places - educational, sports and recreational institutions. As this catalogue has so far been very narrow, there has been a danger that a physiotherapist after a five-year period of work in those facilities which have not been identified as medical agents will lose his right to pursue his profession. Therefore, we are expanding the catalogue of entities in which physiotherapeutic practice can be carried out, and this will mean that it will constitute the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapist. This is a project that is expected by the physiotherapists' environment. It evens the odds, equals the practice of physiotherapist, self-employed physiotherapist, with the rules of other medical professions such as the profession of doctor, dentist, nurse and midwife. The shift of the time limits that were included in the Act gives physiotherapists additional time to prepare for registration of individual, group, or place of call or employer practices. They will also be prepared and introduced into the legal order of the implementing regulation, which will regulate in detail the keeping of registers, the data that should be reported to registers, medical records, the storage of the medical records, as well as detailed rules on the equipment of the physiotherapist's office if it is recorded in the form of a stationary office. All these legal acts will be prepared in such a way that physiotherapists can safely register medicinal products by 1 April 2019 which will enable them to pursue their own physiotherapist profession. One more change concerning the clarification of the conditions that a physiotherapist must fulfil so that he can pursue his profession on his own. Persons who do not comply with the conditions laid down in Article. 4a, i.e. they do not have the right internship, they will be able to pursue the profession at the request of a doctor, as well as at the request of a physiotherapist. These are the changes we are making in this amendment. I ask the High House to accept and adopt these changes. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u353	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles.
