ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u354	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The law of 25 September 2015 on the profession of physiotherapist introduced provisions laying down rules for the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapist as an independent medical profession. With its entry into force, physiotherapists became a professional group subject to similar rules of profession as doctors, dentists, nurses and midwives. However, in part the powers and obligations relating to the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapists are less favourable than those of independent medical professions. This concerns the lack of possibility to pursue a profession in the form of individual and group professional practice. At this time, the situation is detrimental to physiotherapists, a large proportion of whom provide health services in person, often only at the site of the call or at the hospital of another entity. At present, physiotherapists can provide health benefits in the ordinary business. This law changes this disadvantage for physiotherapists by introducing the following solutions. The Act gives the possibility to pursue a profession within the framework of an economic activity in the form of a professional practice with legal status, as in the case of other professions, i.e. in the form of a single-person business activity as: individual physiotherapist practice, individual practice only at the place of call, single-member business activity, individual practice in a medicinal facility on the basis of a contract with a medicinal entity operating the facility or as a civil company, a public company, a partnership. This law indicates that physiotherapists' professional practices will be subject to similar regulations as nurses' practices in meeting the conditions of their medical activities. These are very important elements, very valuable. I'm sure they're very important to physiotherapists. The Act imposes an obligation to include practice in the national register kept by the National Physiotherapists Council. All these solutions are very beneficial for the profession. In particular, I see a particularly positive scope in the area of the extension of the catalogue of activities and positions, the performance of which is also considered to be the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapist. The current catalogue deviates significantly from similar catalogues in other professional authorities and, above all, does not take into account the specifics of the physiotherapist profession, which before the entry into force of the Act was carried out in numerous organisational units without the status of a medicinal entity. This applies in particular to social assistance homes, educational establishments, sports clubs and fitness centres and SPA providers. This will ensure that physiotherapists employed there, often performing activities identical to physiotherapists employed in medicinal entities which are not intended to preserve, rescue, restore or improve health, will not lose their right to provide health services after a period of 5 years of employment in these establishments. The proposed law will have a positive social and economic impact and prevent the negative effects of the current restriction on the conduct of business by physiotherapists and the reduction of the number of providers of health services in the field of physiotherapy. I think this bill is a very expected act. I am convinced that physiotherapists strongly support these changes that they are waiting for. In addition, solutions related to the shifting of these time limits related to registration are very good. This is another element, another way out to meet the expectations of a group of physiotherapists. From this point of view, I would like to assure you that the Law and Justice Club fully supports the proposed law and its solutions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This form of benefit reduces organisational requirements compared to medicinal products and is therefore not only easier to organise, but also cheaper. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u381	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It is my great pleasure to present a bill amending the excise duty refund Act contained in the price of diesel used for agricultural production, contained in print no. 2914. This is the implementation of one of the announcements of the plan for the village - the aid of the Government of Law and Justice for Polish farmers. Let me remind you that helping to reduce the costs of agricultural fuels, which have a great impact on the competitiveness of Polish agriculture, introduced Law and Justice during its first governments, in 2006. This mechanism has worked out, in the opinion of farmers it is strong the support element. We help farmers to reduce the price of the fuel actually purchased for agricultural production on the farm in the most simple way: by returning the funds allocated by the Minister of Finance to the municipalities' offices. We have been analyzing for many years what is happening in terms of diesel consumption on farms. After all, you can see with the naked eye the changes that are taking place, such as the increase in energy consumption in farms, both for vehicle propulsion and for stationary machinery propulsion. In general, agriculture is becoming heavily dependent on energy that is consumed in agricultural production. Therefore, after analysis, as well as consultation with agricultural organisations and after analysis in scientific institutes, which have differing opinions on the amount of fuel consumed in Polish agriculture - this is estimated from 80 to even about 130 l/ha - we are introducing a solution, which is some compromise calculation, which may be extended in the following years, where possible in the budget, of course. We increase the consumption of diesel per hectare of agricultural crops to which excise duty is refunded - from 86 to 100 l/ha of agricultural land. This is to help all those farmers who are users of crops, agricultural land, agricultural land, and it reaches virtually every farm. However, the analysis and votes of farmers indicate that there is an area of agricultural activity where fuel consumption is greater than that of only growing its hectares. This is the production of cattle, and more generally the production of sheep and goats. Although this is a kind of simplification - this mechanism we have applied this year - and there are individual farms dealing with other ruminants, this year, in order not to complicate this mechanism, we propose that only cattle be included, without forgetting, believe me, sheep and goat breeders. Since this year, however, we have introduced a mechanism which is very important, expected by farmers, by cattle producers, namely the refund of excise duty included in the price of diesel for cattle, to each large cattle conversion unit. Let me remind you - farmers probably know them, and if they do not, they are able to master them quickly - special conversion units that allow each piece to be converted according to age into such standard, large conversion units. It's about an appropriate conversion rate for older bulls, for small calves, for heifer at the right age and for cows. We introduce the principle that cattle producers will return the excise duty from 30 l to each large conversion unit. This is really a historic event. It opens a mechanism that we will implement, which will help those who not only grow fields but also carry out energy-intensive production directions, especially in the field of intra-farm transport, the transport of silage, haycombs, bedding, animal manure - all that is required on this farm also in winter, at a time when a typical farm without animal production tractor stands in the garage. This increase in excise duty returns generates significant budgetary costs. To cover the costs of this excise duty refund in the Budget Act for 2019 we reserved PLN 1180 million. I will remind you that in the year passing in the budget is for this - and this is enough - 911 million PLN. That's over 300 million, in addition, compared to this mechanism that we introduced many years ago, which worked out. The Act does not raise any particular controversy, I believe, but also does not require particular knowledge to determine how the farmer will calculate the amount of return. These are hectares which are in the land register, 100 l and are fixed once a year, by the end of December, the rate of return. This mechanism is a little more complicated, but objective. It must be used to ensure that there is no abuse of the return mechanism for bovine animals. This is the determination of the average annual state of the large conversion units on the holding. Nor should there be any particular problems here, because the animal registration system operated by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture is an objective source of data that a farmer can obtain from the county restructuring agency's office at any time. This will be done by submitting an application by the farmer and issuing an appropriate document by the head of the district office, which will determine the amount of large conversion units that are entitled to excise duty refund in a very simple way. Namely, it is the number of large conversion units per last day of each month per year. It is also a matter of not hurting anyone, because there are also movements of the herd, there are sales, new calves are born, farmers buy cattle during the year. This is therefore a dynamic process and this needs to be taken into account. So the rule will be this: determining on the basis of the units the actual times the ratios of the large conversion units on the last day of each month; the sum of these 12 months must be divided by 12 and the average annual state of the large conversion units for which this 30-litre return will apply. It was also consulted with agricultural organisations, it is well received. Some say that maybe in the next few years you'd have to try to raise the volume of gallons. This isn't a closed trial. On the other hand, I am convinced - and I want my satisfaction, my joy to share with you - that this is a very good step in the right direction. I ask the High House to adopt a draft law amending the excise duty refund Act contained in the price of diesel used for agricultural production, contained in print No 2914. Thank you very much. The applicants are putting us in a pretty difficult position. Unacceptance of this project in their intention means opposition to the need to celebrate the 100th anniversary of Poland's regaining independence in 1918. But that's not true. We worship this anniversary in different ways and do not need any weirdness in this regard. We see the effort and tribute of blood and life which those who brought and established Poland on the altar of Polish freedom gave to them, and the memory of the events connected with it passed on to the next generations. However, the vague argument of the applicants cannot be accepted that the bill will promote longer celebrations, as this argument contains a fundamental logical error. The Act excludes, says which entities will be able to operate, about large-area shops. Does the bill have a positive social impact? For who? For those you're excluding from the celebrations, working in... I'm not gonna name those entities, not to be charged with advertising them. Is celebrating a national celebration on the occasion of the 100th anniversary of regaining independence supposed to be about supplying trade, including networks of large commercial and service corporations? Do you want to compensate them for the lack of profits on statutory days excluded from large-area trade? Or do you want to establish a new tradition of celebration - in commercial warehouses? With regard to the draft law, it must be noted that there is no logic in the argument that the establishment of a day off from all work except trade does not allow a measurable determination of the consequences for the state budget. It's so simple that you don't even need the finance minister's knowledge to figure it out. To sum up, despite our general principle, and we are in favour of directing draft laws and resolutions to continue to proceed at the meetings of the Sejm or in committees, in this case as Kukiz,15 we make an exception, but in line with our logic, that the draft laws harmful to the Polish society and absurd should be rejected, we apply for the rejection of the draft law at first reading, which is to be recorded and put to the vote in the voting block. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Now.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u569	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On 20 October this year. in the auditorium of John Paul II of the Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński University in Warsaw in the framework of the 2nd International Movement Congress, the conference entitled "The Strong Family Force of Europe" took place. About 300 people from different parts of Poland took part in the conference. The speakers were high representatives of the Catholic Church, science and politics from Poland, Croatia, Spain, Lithuania and Italy. In the narrow time frame of my statement, I am unable to quote a number of significant threads during the programme speeches, so I will limit myself only to presenting to the High Sejm the position which was adopted at the end of the session, believing that the High House will share the views expressed by the participants of this significant event. The final position of the participants of the conference "Strong family by force of Europe" organized in the framework of the Second International Movement Congress "Europe Christi" in cooperation with the Parliamentary Group for Catholic Social Science and the Polish Family Rights Committee, Warsaw on 20 October 2018. The participants of the conference take the view that the family is natural, primal to others and the basic social cell. The family is a priceless value that must be properly protected both nationally and internationally. Marriage is regarded as a voluntary relationship between women and men in accordance with formal rules is the foundation of the family and its sustainability should be protected to the full. The natural and irreplaceable environment of the proper development of the child from conception to the attainment of adulthood is the family, and any other solutions in this regard pose a threat to the proper development of the child. Family rights are natural, and their recognition by all authorities, communities and public opinion is a fundamental and inalienable obligation. The Charter of Family Rights proclaimed by the Holy See on 22 October 1983, irrespective of the views of politicians, should inspire all authorities responsible for establishing and respecting the rights of the general and local population. The participants of the conference appeal to the state and local authorities, international and ecclesiastical institutions and social activists and all people of good will to increase their concern for the subjectivity of the family by continuing to take various actions aimed at the well-being of the family and each of its members, creating conditions for the proper functioning and comprehensive development of the family and each person, stigmatizing actions that pose a threat to the family, especially in the legal, economic, social and moral spheres. Participants of the conference concerned about the future of the family and about the creation and respect of its rights call for special protection of marriage, family and parenthood from all competent authorities in Poland, Europe and the world by taking urgent work to ratify the International Convention on Family Rights, establishing on 22 October an international day of family rights, creating and defending family-friendly rights in every dimension of social and individual life. Above all, we ask the Sejm, the Senate and the President of the Republic of Poland to decide as soon as possible, which will safeguard the legal protection of life from conception to natural death, so that the signatures of nearly 900 thousand. Polish citizens under the citizens' bill "Stop abortion" received proper respect, and God's law - do not kill anyone became applicable in Poland. The conference participants express their gratitude to all institutions and authorities and to all people of good will for the actions they have taken so far in the care of marriage, family and everyone from conception to natural death. On behalf of the participants of the conference, Fr. Inf. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u571	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As a Member from Pomerania, I would like to ask about Pomerania's investments. At the previous session of the Sejm, we started a discussion on our country's budget for 2019. Unfortunately, this is not a good budget for the inhabitants of Pomeranian Voivodship. I would like to ask why the government in the draft budget law for 2019 did not foresee funds for the construction of the Tri-City Metropolitan Circuit. This is one of the most important investments for inhabitants of Pomeranian Voivodship. The European Commission has already pointed out to the Polish government that the construction of the Metropolitan Circuit of the Tricity is a key element of the whole S6 route co-financed by European funds. This year, the administrative court rejected the complaint against the environmental decision and became final. The PiS government claimed that when the NSA's decision is made, then the construction of the metropolitan ring road will begin. The current regional Tricity has actually become the road of the inner agglomeration, the urban road, and it is already very crowded. And that's not gonna happen. Despite the positive decision of the court in the 2019 budget, not even a zloty was foreseen for the construction of the Tri-City Metropolitan Circuit. Of course, I know about two complaints currently filed with the NSA, but they will be settled on 14 November. I therefore call very much for funds to be secured for this important route for the entire agglomeration, as well as for all the people of our country who are going to the sea and there are a lot of them every year. I call on the government to find money and save money in the budget for 2019 for the Tri-City Metropolitan Ridge. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u573	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Last Sunday there were elections to local authorities throughout Poland. That's not the end of it. We have been repeatedly accused of the intention to forge elections, of political persecutions, of everything that may be an argument in the attack by foreign opposition activists' partners whose interests are threatened, when the Polish state improves its institutions, effectively pursues when it comes to unlawful financial transfers and tax fraud. But let's see what the practice of these elections looked like. The State Election Commission reported a significantly lower number of incidents due to the violation of electoral law than in 2014. The percentage of non-valid votes instead of a dozen percent was at most a few percent, as is the case with normal, well-managed elections. That's all with a turnout of not 47%, but 55%. High Izbo! This is what a real celebration of democracy looks like. Elections that have been organized and conducted better for the first time in years than before. The computer system wasn't suspended, and most of the results are already known. So much remains in contact with reality from the evil, antagonizing and simply lying propaganda of the opposition, which itself called itself total. If something in today's Poland poses a real threat to democracy, it is only their contempt for the results of public decisions, especially if the result does not meet their expectations. Law and Justice always respect the voices of society. As you can see from the preliminary analysis of the electoral process, it is a guarantee of fairness. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-23-sejm-70-1.u575	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! These are questions for the Prime Minister. Dear Prime Minister, I am astonished to see the passiveness of state bodies that have not taken action to clarify the case concerning former Deputy Minister of Foreign Affairs in the government of Prime Minister Beata Szydło Robert Grey. The matter has not been properly clarified either to the public or to the interested person himself, as evidenced by the speech by Mr Robert Winnicki of 9 May this year. Robert Grey, at the end of his duties as a senior state official in the Ministry led by Witold Waszczykowski, was subjected to an unprecedented attack by the Polish-dominated press titles, among others. Wyborcza gazety and Faktu, with which he is pursuing processes for the protection of personal goods. Given the scale of the accusations against the former Deputy Minister, I ask the Prime Minister the following questions: Did the relevant state authorities take any action to explain the allegations against Robert Grey, in particular to the extent that the Polish media were to obtain information concerning Minister Grey from the widely understood services operating in the country? Did the then Foreign Minister Witold Waszczykowski take action to clarify the allegations against one of his key subordinates, in particular did he speak with him personally and did he hold a press conference on the subject of the circumstances concerning the termination of cooperation and media speculation with Mr Grey? Did the then foreign minister Witold Waszczykowski make changes in the management and in the areas he pointed out after Robert Grey's cancellation? Did the Foreign Minister of that time carry out a media statement that he would use Mr Grey's experience in a different way? Was Minister Grey offered a suitable job at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, which Witold Waszczykowski then announced in the media or any work at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs? What were the actions of the then President of the Council of Ministers, Beata Szydło, in order to explain to Poles the existence or not of the grounds of the allegations directed against a high-ranking government official, in particular whether an interview was held with Robert Grey and whether explanations were received from Witold Waszczykowski? Is there any protocol in place? Has a press conference been held to protect the image of Poland both in the country and in the international arena? What actions were undertaken by Minister Mariusz Kaminski regarding the verification of information allegedly given to the media by the Polish services concerning Robert Grey? Have ABW, SKW, BBN and other government agencies taken action to clarify the case? If so, what is it? What effect did they have? What action has been taken to clarify the claim made by the Minister's company to receive PLN 24 million? Was that information true? Did the CBA take appropriate action? If so, what is it? If not, why not? Following Mr Winnicki's statement in the Sejm on 9 May 2018, have measures been taken to clarify the questions raised in the statement? If any of the above applies. action has been taken, and where can it be seen of its effects and findings? Are there procedures for the state's response to such cases? Has any action been taken to prevent this from happening in the future? Dear Prime Minister, On behalf of Mr Robert Grey, I would like to ask you very much to take a closer look at the matter and to examine in depth all the events that have taken place in the past and undoubtedly the tragic effects that have been reflected on the present by the former Deputy Minister of Foreign Affairs. Thank you very much.
